Protocol for iterative enrichment of integrated sgRNAs via derivative CRISPR-Cas9 libraries from genomic DNA of sorted fixed cells
Authors: Adriana Ordóñez, David Ron, Heather P. Harding
Published: 2024

ABSTRACT
SummaryHere, we present a protocol for iterative enrichment of integrated single guide RNA (sgRNA) via derivative CRISPR-Cas9 from genomic DNA (gDNA) of phenotypically sorted fixed cells. We describe steps for high-scale lentiviral production, genome-wide screening, extracting gDNA from fixed cells, cloning of integrated sgRNAs, and high-scale transformation. This protocol introduces three key advantages: (1) applicability to fixed cells, (2) bypassing epigenetic drift, and (3) pause points lowering the contamination risk. We believe this approach will benefit researchers applying somatic cell genetics in cell biology.For complete details on the use and execution of this protocol, please refer to Ordoñez et al.1

Before you begin

The protocol below outlines the specific steps for working with adherent Chinese Hamster Ovary (CHO-K1; ATCC CCL-61) cells. It is noteworthy that we have successfully applied a similar approach in human HeLa cells.

CRISPR-Cas9 screens are powerful techniques for unbiased genetic target discovery. Conventionally, live cells exhibiting phenotypes induced by integrated single guide RNAs (sgRNAs) are sorted and subjected to multiple rounds of positive selection and expansion. This process enhances the sensitivity for detecting targeted genes with low expression or subtle effects. This is followed by deep sequencing to identify enriched sgRNAs.2,3,4 Though valuable, this approach has limitations, as it requires live cells, carries the risk of (epi)genetic drift5 and poses a high contamination risk due to the continuous nature of cell expansion, without pause points.

Here, we present a comprehensive protocol for CRISPR-Cas9 screens compatible with fixed cells. The protocol involves selecting cells with desired phenotypes by Fluorescence-Activated Cell Sorting (FACS), recovering sgRNA sequences from the genomic DNA (gDNA) of phenotypically sorted fixed cells and generating phenotype-linked sgRNA derivative libraries. These libraries are subsequently used for additional rounds of transduction and sorting. This approach enriches sgRNA directly from fixed cell pellets, offering three key advantages: compatibility with fixed cells, reduced (epi)genetic drift, and lower contamination risk due to build-in pause-points (see flow diagram in Figure 1).Figure 1Flow diagrams depicting the two CRISPR-Cas9 enrichment techniques mentioned in the main textMethod A illustrates the conventional enrichment of single guide RNAs (sgRNA) from live cells, which involves Fluorescence-Activated Cell Sorting (FACS) and the expansion of phenotypically-selected live cells.Method B presents our proposed enrichment method, involving FACS of fixed cells and extraction of genomic DNA (gDNA) from fixed sorted cells to generate derivative enriched libraries. In Method B, the green sections indicate the introduction of convenient pause points.

Flow diagrams depicting the two CRISPR-Cas9 enrichment techniques mentioned in the main text

Method A illustrates the conventional enrichment of single guide RNAs (sgRNA) from live cells, which involves Fluorescence-Activated Cell Sorting (FACS) and the expansion of phenotypically-selected live cells.

Method B presents our proposed enrichment method, involving FACS of fixed cells and extraction of genomic DNA (gDNA) from fixed sorted cells to generate derivative enriched libraries. In Method B, the green sections indicate the introduction of convenient pause points.

This protocol was originally developed to identify genes affecting intracellular trafficking of a polymerogenic alpha1-antitrypsin (α1AT)H334D variant. Polymers were tracked by sorting fixed and permeabilized cells stained with a polymer-specific monoclonal antibody (Mab2C1).1,6

In the first section, we will review the preparatory steps before initiating the high-throughput CRISPR-Cas9 screen. This includes the generation and characterization of Cas9 stable cell lines, assessing the dynamic range of the assay, identifying critical parameters and designing the experimental layout.

Overall, this protocol provides a flow cytometry-based high-throughput screening method that is compatible with fixed cells that can be managed with relatively standard laboratory resources.

Institutional permissions

As lentiviruses are utilized in this protocol, ensure all procedures are approved by the Institutional Biosafety Committee. The individual conducting the experiments must be proficient in Biosafety Level 2 (BSL-2) tissue culture work. All virus production must be carried out under the guidelines for BSL-2 laboratory.

Experimental design considerations

It is essential to establish a reliable readout cell line for FACS sorting and perform small-scale pilot experiments to confirm its suitability for CRISPR-Cas9 screens. Familiarize yourself and conduct pilot experiments with relevant techniques: flow cytometry, gDNA extraction, multi-layer flask culturing, cell fixation, permeabilization and cloning. Acquire the necessary flasks, plates, cell culture medium and reagents in advance and ensure your incubators have sufficient capacity for the required number of flasks. Given the labor-intensive nature of this protocol, particularly the cell culture component, it is recommended that it not be performed by a single person. Teaming with a knowledgeable colleague as a lab partner is highly advisable in case of unexpected absences.

Generate Cas9-expressing cells and cell characterization

Timing: 5–6 weeks

Timing: 5–6 weeks

The CRISPR-Cas9 system comprises two fundamental elements: the Cas9 endonuclease and the targeting sgRNAs. Both can be introduced together or separately via lentiviral transduction. Our study presents a pooled CRISPR-Cas9 screen wherein a sgRNA lentiviral plasmid library (manuscript in preparation), targeting the entire CHO-K1 genome, is delivered to a pool of CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D Cas9-expressing cells via viral transduction.1.Produce lentivirus on a small scale.a.Plate 3.6 × 105 HEK 293T (Human embryonic kidney 293T cells) cells per 60 mm culture dish in 4 mL of HEK 293T DMEM complete medium to achieve 50% confluence on the day of transfection. Gently swirl the plate in an "eight shape" or shake it back to evenly distribute the cells.Note: HEK 293T cells detach easily, practise caution when adding or removing medium or Dulbecco’s phosphate-buffered saline (DPBS).b.The following day, change the medium and transfect with LentiCas9 plasmid (UK1714) along with the packaging plasmid psPAX2 (UK1701; Addgene #12260) and envelope plasmid pMD2.G (UK1700; Addgene #12259) at a 10:7.5:5 ratio using TransIT-293 (MIR2700), following the manufacturer’s instructions: https://tools.mirusbio.com/assets/protocols/ml009_transit_293_transfection_reagent.pdfNote: We use a LentiCas9 plasmid (UK1714), modified from the LentiCas9-Blasticidine (Blast) (UK1674; Addgene #52962) without its Blast resistance marker, as the recipient cells are already Blast resistant (from the introduction of the Tet-on_α1ATH334D transgene).c.The next day, replace the medium with 4 mL DMEM complete medium supplemented with 1% v/v filtered bovine serum albumin (BSA) and incubate for another 24 h at 37°C with 5% CO2.Note: Prepare a 0.22 μm filtered, sterile 10% w/v BSA stock solution in water and add to the medium for a final concentration of 1% v/v. BSA enhances the stability, yield, and quality of lentivirus particles.d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect medium containing lentivirus.i.Transfer the medium to a 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tube.ii.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet cell debris.iii.Filter the supernatant through a 0.45 μm surfactant-free cellulose acetate (SFCA) membrane.Note: Use immediately or store at 4°C for up to two weeks (with loss in titer).CRITICAL: Inactivate any lentivirus in waste medium using a suitable agent (e.g., 2% Virkon or Vernagel absorbent powder) before disposal.2.Transduce CHO-K1 recipient cells to generate a Cas9 stable cell line.a.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells per well in a 6-well plate with CHO Ham’s F12 medium, using three wells in total.b.Incubate at 37°C for 24 h to achieve 80% confluence.c.After 24 h, replace the medium and proceed with transductions:i.For lentivirus infection, add 100 μL (low MOI) or 500 μL (high MOI) of lentivirus-containing medium collected in step 1d, along with 2 μg/mL polybrene (PB; Hexadimethrine bromide).Note: Polybrene enhances lentiviral infection by facilitating viral entry.ii.For the control well, simply replace the medium without virus.3.Isolate potential Cas9 clones using limiting dilution cloning (LDC).a.Remove the medium and wash the wells with 1 mL DPBS.b.Add 0.5 mL of pre-warmed 1x trypsin-EDTA cell dissociation reagent and incubate for 3–5 min at 37°C.c.Add 0.5 mL of pre-warmed CHO Ham’s F12 medium to neutralize the trypsin and pipette to break up the cell aggregates.d.Transfer the entire cell suspension (1 mL) to a 100 mm dish containing 9 mL of medium. This is a 1:10 dilution.e.Perform serial dilutions to 1:100, 1:1,000 and 1:10,000.f.Incubate and replace medium every 2–3 days.g.After a week of growth, isolated colonies are usually visible on the highly diluted plates (e.g., 1:1,000 or 1:10,000). Pick well-isolated colonies for expansion.i.Use a fine marker to mark the bottom of the dish where each colony is located.ii.Using a 20 μL pipette, scrape the isolated colonies and gently collect the cells. Deposit the cells into a new well of a 12- or 24-well plate with growth medium, leaving the clumps to spread on their own.iii.Allow clones to grow. Expand at least 24 single clones into 24-well plates.iv.Once the clones reach 50–80% confluence, split them into two wells of 12- or 6-well plates: one for Cas9 stable integration analysis and the other to keep as a stock for further expansion.4.Validate Cas9 integration. Cas9 integration can be confirmed by measuring its expression and/or activity. In this protocol, Cas9 integration is validated by activity since CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells also harbor a green fluorescent protein (GFP) reporter for monitoring the unfolded protein response (UPR). Cells are transfected with a GFP-targeting sgRNA (UK1717) and UPR is induced with 2.5 μg/mL Tunicamycin for 24 h. Flow cytometry measures GFP expression and cells with active Cas9 exhibit reduced GFP fluorescence (Figure 2).Note: If the cells lack a fluorescent reporter, Cas9 expression can be evaluated via immunoblotting, or function can be tested by targeting a selectable endogenous gene, such as HPRT.75.Perform cell line characterization.To adapt this protocol to other cell lines, it is essential to optimize the following conditions.a.Select the appropriate cell culture medium and determine the doubling time. For CHO-K1 cells cultured in CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium, the double time is typically between 18-22 h, but it is recommended to confirm this before screening.Note: Determining the doubling time is especially important for large-scale screening, as it allows accurate estimation of cell population at different stages of the screening.b.Determine the optimal seeding number. High-throughput CRISPR screening implies different seeding formats, from 6 well-plates to 150 mm dishes and multi-flasks. Create a seeding table to ensure accurate cell densities. For CHO-K1 cells, use the provided table.Culturing formatcm2Seed for 80%–85% confluence in 24 hCulture medium volume (mL)T875- 5 multi-layer flasks8751.5 × 107150T1751753.0 × 10630150 mm1502.6 × 10626100 mm591.0 × 1061060 mm213.6 × 1053.66-well plate101.6 × 1051.6c.Determine puromycin sensitivity: The sgRNA library lentiviral plasmid (pKLV-CHO_libA-PGKpuro2ABFP) for the CRISPR knockout screen incorporates a puromycin resistance marker and a blue fluorescent (BFP) marker to allow both selection and flow cytometry screening. Test puromycin sensitivity as follows:i.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1 cells in 6-well plates with 2 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 medium.ii.After 48 h, add increasing puromycin concentrations (1, 4, 6, 8, 10 μg/mL).iii.Grow cells for 2–3 days.iv.Select the lowest concentration that kills 100% of cells in 2–3 days.Note: For CHO-K1 cells, the typical puromycin concentration used is 8 μg/mL.d.Determinate polybrene (PB) toxicity. Test PB toxicity similarly, with concentrations of 0.002, 0.02, 2, 4, 8 μg/mL.Note: The optimal PB concentration is typically established through empirical testing, usually within the 2–12 μg/mL range, without being toxic to the cells. For CHO-K1 cells, 2 μg/mL is commonly used.CRITICAL: Always include a control group with no antibiotic treatment during optimizations.Figure 2Dot-histogram plot confirming Cas9 stable integration in the cell line used in the CRISPR-Cas9 screenCHO-α1ATH334D/Cas9 cells harboring an endogenous fluorescent CHOP::GFP reporter were analyzed by flow cytometry under basal conditions (UT, red) or following treatment with Tunicamycin (Tm) alone (blue) or transfection with an individual lentiGuide-Puro plasmid (without expression of Cas9) targeting GFP (UK1717, orange). ER stress treatments with 2.5 μg/mL Tm lasted 24 h. The GFP high expression population that remained after transfection with a GFP-targeting sgRNA (orange) and treatment with Tm represents cells that either have not incorporated the GFP-targeting sgRNA or have not caused frameshift mutation.

1.Produce lentivirus on a small scale.a.Plate 3.6 × 105 HEK 293T (Human embryonic kidney 293T cells) cells per 60 mm culture dish in 4 mL of HEK 293T DMEM complete medium to achieve 50% confluence on the day of transfection. Gently swirl the plate in an "eight shape" or shake it back to evenly distribute the cells.Note: HEK 293T cells detach easily, practise caution when adding or removing medium or Dulbecco’s phosphate-buffered saline (DPBS).b.The following day, change the medium and transfect with LentiCas9 plasmid (UK1714) along with the packaging plasmid psPAX2 (UK1701; Addgene #12260) and envelope plasmid pMD2.G (UK1700; Addgene #12259) at a 10:7.5:5 ratio using TransIT-293 (MIR2700), following the manufacturer’s instructions: https://tools.mirusbio.com/assets/protocols/ml009_transit_293_transfection_reagent.pdfNote: We use a LentiCas9 plasmid (UK1714), modified from the LentiCas9-Blasticidine (Blast) (UK1674; Addgene #52962) without its Blast resistance marker, as the recipient cells are already Blast resistant (from the introduction of the Tet-on_α1ATH334D transgene).c.The next day, replace the medium with 4 mL DMEM complete medium supplemented with 1% v/v filtered bovine serum albumin (BSA) and incubate for another 24 h at 37°C with 5% CO2.Note: Prepare a 0.22 μm filtered, sterile 10% w/v BSA stock solution in water and add to the medium for a final concentration of 1% v/v. BSA enhances the stability, yield, and quality of lentivirus particles.d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect medium containing lentivirus.i.Transfer the medium to a 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tube.ii.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet cell debris.iii.Filter the supernatant through a 0.45 μm surfactant-free cellulose acetate (SFCA) membrane.Note: Use immediately or store at 4°C for up to two weeks (with loss in titer).CRITICAL: Inactivate any lentivirus in waste medium using a suitable agent (e.g., 2% Virkon or Vernagel absorbent powder) before disposal.

d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect medium containing lentivirus.i.Transfer the medium to a 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tube.ii.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet cell debris.iii.Filter the supernatant through a 0.45 μm surfactant-free cellulose acetate (SFCA) membrane.Note: Use immediately or store at 4°C for up to two weeks (with loss in titer).CRITICAL: Inactivate any lentivirus in waste medium using a suitable agent (e.g., 2% Virkon or Vernagel absorbent powder) before disposal.

2.Transduce CHO-K1 recipient cells to generate a Cas9 stable cell line.a.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells per well in a 6-well plate with CHO Ham’s F12 medium, using three wells in total.b.Incubate at 37°C for 24 h to achieve 80% confluence.c.After 24 h, replace the medium and proceed with transductions:i.For lentivirus infection, add 100 μL (low MOI) or 500 μL (high MOI) of lentivirus-containing medium collected in step 1d, along with 2 μg/mL polybrene (PB; Hexadimethrine bromide).Note: Polybrene enhances lentiviral infection by facilitating viral entry.ii.For the control well, simply replace the medium without virus.

3.Isolate potential Cas9 clones using limiting dilution cloning (LDC).a.Remove the medium and wash the wells with 1 mL DPBS.b.Add 0.5 mL of pre-warmed 1x trypsin-EDTA cell dissociation reagent and incubate for 3–5 min at 37°C.c.Add 0.5 mL of pre-warmed CHO Ham’s F12 medium to neutralize the trypsin and pipette to break up the cell aggregates.d.Transfer the entire cell suspension (1 mL) to a 100 mm dish containing 9 mL of medium. This is a 1:10 dilution.e.Perform serial dilutions to 1:100, 1:1,000 and 1:10,000.f.Incubate and replace medium every 2–3 days.g.After a week of growth, isolated colonies are usually visible on the highly diluted plates (e.g., 1:1,000 or 1:10,000). Pick well-isolated colonies for expansion.i.Use a fine marker to mark the bottom of the dish where each colony is located.ii.Using a 20 μL pipette, scrape the isolated colonies and gently collect the cells. Deposit the cells into a new well of a 12- or 24-well plate with growth medium, leaving the clumps to spread on their own.iii.Allow clones to grow. Expand at least 24 single clones into 24-well plates.iv.Once the clones reach 50–80% confluence, split them into two wells of 12- or 6-well plates: one for Cas9 stable integration analysis and the other to keep as a stock for further expansion.

ii.Using a 20 μL pipette, scrape the isolated colonies and gently collect the cells. Deposit the cells into a new well of a 12- or 24-well plate with growth medium, leaving the clumps to spread on their own.

Using a 20 μL pipette, scrape the isolated colonies and gently collect the cells. Deposit the cells into a new well of a 12- or 24-well plate with growth medium, leaving the clumps to spread on their own.

iii.Allow clones to grow. Expand at least 24 single clones into 24-well plates.

Allow clones to grow. Expand at least 24 single clones into 24-well plates.

iv.Once the clones reach 50–80% confluence, split them into two wells of 12- or 6-well plates: one for Cas9 stable integration analysis and the other to keep as a stock for further expansion.

Once the clones reach 50–80% confluence, split them into two wells of 12- or 6-well plates: one for Cas9 stable integration analysis and the other to keep as a stock for further expansion.

4.Validate Cas9 integration. Cas9 integration can be confirmed by measuring its expression and/or activity. In this protocol, Cas9 integration is validated by activity since CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells also harbor a green fluorescent protein (GFP) reporter for monitoring the unfolded protein response (UPR). Cells are transfected with a GFP-targeting sgRNA (UK1717) and UPR is induced with 2.5 μg/mL Tunicamycin for 24 h. Flow cytometry measures GFP expression and cells with active Cas9 exhibit reduced GFP fluorescence (Figure 2).

Validate Cas9 integration. Cas9 integration can be confirmed by measuring its expression and/or activity. In this protocol, Cas9 integration is validated by activity since CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells also harbor a green fluorescent protein (GFP) reporter for monitoring the unfolded protein response (UPR). Cells are transfected with a GFP-targeting sgRNA (UK1717) and UPR is induced with 2.5 μg/mL Tunicamycin for 24 h. Flow cytometry measures GFP expression and cells with active Cas9 exhibit reduced GFP fluorescence (Figure 2).

Note: If the cells lack a fluorescent reporter, Cas9 expression can be evaluated via immunoblotting, or function can be tested by targeting a selectable endogenous gene, such as HPRT.7

5.Perform cell line characterization.To adapt this protocol to other cell lines, it is essential to optimize the following conditions.a.Select the appropriate cell culture medium and determine the doubling time. For CHO-K1 cells cultured in CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium, the double time is typically between 18-22 h, but it is recommended to confirm this before screening.Note: Determining the doubling time is especially important for large-scale screening, as it allows accurate estimation of cell population at different stages of the screening.b.Determine the optimal seeding number. High-throughput CRISPR screening implies different seeding formats, from 6 well-plates to 150 mm dishes and multi-flasks. Create a seeding table to ensure accurate cell densities. For CHO-K1 cells, use the provided table.Culturing formatcm2Seed for 80%–85% confluence in 24 hCulture medium volume (mL)T875- 5 multi-layer flasks8751.5 × 107150T1751753.0 × 10630150 mm1502.6 × 10626100 mm591.0 × 1061060 mm213.6 × 1053.66-well plate101.6 × 1051.6c.Determine puromycin sensitivity: The sgRNA library lentiviral plasmid (pKLV-CHO_libA-PGKpuro2ABFP) for the CRISPR knockout screen incorporates a puromycin resistance marker and a blue fluorescent (BFP) marker to allow both selection and flow cytometry screening. Test puromycin sensitivity as follows:i.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1 cells in 6-well plates with 2 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 medium.ii.After 48 h, add increasing puromycin concentrations (1, 4, 6, 8, 10 μg/mL).iii.Grow cells for 2–3 days.iv.Select the lowest concentration that kills 100% of cells in 2–3 days.Note: For CHO-K1 cells, the typical puromycin concentration used is 8 μg/mL.d.Determinate polybrene (PB) toxicity. Test PB toxicity similarly, with concentrations of 0.002, 0.02, 2, 4, 8 μg/mL.Note: The optimal PB concentration is typically established through empirical testing, usually within the 2–12 μg/mL range, without being toxic to the cells. For CHO-K1 cells, 2 μg/mL is commonly used.CRITICAL: Always include a control group with no antibiotic treatment during optimizations.

Note: The optimal PB concentration is typically established through empirical testing, usually within the 2–12 μg/mL range, without being toxic to the cells. For CHO-K1 cells, 2 μg/mL is commonly used.

CRITICAL: Always include a control group with no antibiotic treatment during optimizations.

Dot-histogram plot confirming Cas9 stable integration in the cell line used in the CRISPR-Cas9 screen

CHO-α1ATH334D/Cas9 cells harboring an endogenous fluorescent CHOP::GFP reporter were analyzed by flow cytometry under basal conditions (UT, red) or following treatment with Tunicamycin (Tm) alone (blue) or transfection with an individual lentiGuide-Puro plasmid (without expression of Cas9) targeting GFP (UK1717, orange). ER stress treatments with 2.5 μg/mL Tm lasted 24 h. The GFP high expression population that remained after transfection with a GFP-targeting sgRNA (orange) and treatment with Tm represents cells that either have not incorporated the GFP-targeting sgRNA or have not caused frameshift mutation.

Determine assay dynamic range on small scale: Fixation, permeabilization, and intracellular staining of α1AT polymers for FACS analysis

Timing: 2–3 weeks

Timing: 2–3 weeks

Success of in high-throughput screening is affected by the dynamic range of the readout. A wider fluorescence intensity range enhances screening confidence. This protocol uses CHO-K1 Cas9 stable cells expressing α1AT polymers under a Tet-on inducible promoter.1,8 To assess the dynamic range of polymer levels detectable by FACS in response to genetic perturbation via CRISPR-Cas9 screen, we will assess the increase in intracellular Mab2C1-stained polymers, after treatment with doxycycline to identify the optimal concentration for screening [see Figure S1 in Ordoñez et al. (2021)1].6.Determinate doxycycline concentration needed to induce polymer-staining.a.Seed 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1 cells per well in 6 well-plates using Ham’s F12 complete medium. Use two plates to test at least 10 conditions.b.After 48 h, replace the medium with fresh medium containing increasing concentration of doxycycline (from 5 ng/mL to 1000 ng/mL).c.Incubate the plates at 37°C for 24 h.7.Fix and permeabilize cells for intracellular polymer staining and flow cytometry.This section outlines the fixation of cells prior to intracellular staining to stabilize soluble antigens and preserve their cellular location. It also covers the permeabilization process. Keep in mind that permeabilization alters light scatter profiles during flow cytometer. This protocol is designed for CHO-K1 cells from 6-well plates, with an approximately 6 × 105 cells. For high-throughput screening, increase cell counts and reagent volumes as specified in the step-by-step protocol.a.Aspirate medium.b.Wash cells twice with 1 mL of DPBS.c.Add 1 mL of 4 mM EDTA-DPBS supplemented with 0.2% BSA. Gently pipette up and down using a 1 mL pipette.Note: Use 4 mM EDTA-DPBS to detach cells for FACS instead of trypsin, as it preserves cell surface markers, viability and sample quality.Note: Adding BSA helps to reduce cell aggregation during FACS. This is crucial for large-scale sorting (where cells will be sorted for several hours) but may be optional for small-scale sorting.d.Transfer the cell suspension into a 1.5 mL low-protein-binding tubes.Note: Low-binding tubes are essential, as fixation and permeabilization will make cells sticky.e.Without centrifugation, fix cells by adding 37% formaldehyde to achieve a final concentration of 1% v/v.f.Incubate with gentle orbital rotation at room temperature (20°C–25°C) for 10 min.Note: This protocol is designed for subsequent gDNA extraction. For optimal genomic DNA quality, it is important not to exceed the formaldehyde concentration or incubation time.CRITICAL: Wear PPE (lab coat, safety glasses, gloves) and handle formaldehyde in a fume hood.g.Centrifuge cells at 700 ×g for 5 min at 20°C–25°C.h.Carefully aspirate supernatant. Use a 10 μL tip on a 1 mL pipette to minimize cell loss.Pause point: If needed, add DPBS with 0.2% BSA and store at 4°C with gentle rocking. The next day, centrifuge and remove the supernatant.i.Resuspend in 0.5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer and incubate with gentle orbital for 20 min at 20°C–25°C.j.Centrifuge at 700 ×g for 5 min and carefully aspirate the supernatant.k.Resuspend the cell pellet in ∼150 μL of primary antibody diluted in permeabilization/blocking buffer. Incubate for 45 min at 20°C–25°C with orbital rotation.Note: For α1AT polymer detection, use Mab2C1 at 1 μg/mL.Note: Although Mab2C1 has been well-characterized, this is the first protocol detailing its use in flow cytometry; concentration and incubation times were optimized to favor signal over background.l.Wash cells 3 times with 1 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer by centrifugation at 700 ×g for 5 min, then resuspend.m.Dilute the fluorochrome-labeled secondary antibody (Goat anti-Mouse, DyLight 633) in permeabilization/blocking buffer (1:1,000). Resuspend cells in this solution (200 μL) and incubate for 30 min at 20°C–25°C in the dark.Note: Include three controls to determinate the background fluorescence by omitting the primary, secondary, or both antibodies.n.Wash cells 4 times with 1 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer.o.Resuspend cells in ice-cold DPBS and store in the dark at 4°C. For large samples, resuspend in DPBS with 2% BSA.p.Analyze the samples by flow cytometry as soon as possible, ideally the same or following day. After 3 days, the fluorescence signal begins to decay.Note: In this study, cells were treated with a range of doxycycline concentrations, both in the presence or absence of BafilomycinA1, an inhibitor of lysosomal activity that increases α1AT polymer accumulation9 and is useful for exploring the dynamic range. The concentration of doxycycline associated with low basal levels of Mab2C1 staining, which increased conspicuously upon BafilomycinA1 treatment, was selected as the screening condition [see Supplemental Figure S1 in Ordoñez et al. (2021)1]. The doxycycline concentration chosen for screening was 10 ng/mL.Note: This example is for tetracycline-induced gene expression but can be adapted for other treatments. Consider testing fixed drug concentrations over time or a dose curve within standard working hours for high-throughput screening.CRITICAL: It is essential to gently perform multiple pipetting cycles when resuspending the cellular pellets to reduce the presence of doublets, which are excluded from flow cytometry analysis. While minor clumps may not significantly affect small-scale experiments, minimizing clumping is crucial in larger-scale screenings.

7.Fix and permeabilize cells for intracellular polymer staining and flow cytometry.This section outlines the fixation of cells prior to intracellular staining to stabilize soluble antigens and preserve their cellular location. It also covers the permeabilization process. Keep in mind that permeabilization alters light scatter profiles during flow cytometer. This protocol is designed for CHO-K1 cells from 6-well plates, with an approximately 6 × 105 cells. For high-throughput screening, increase cell counts and reagent volumes as specified in the step-by-step protocol.a.Aspirate medium.b.Wash cells twice with 1 mL of DPBS.c.Add 1 mL of 4 mM EDTA-DPBS supplemented with 0.2% BSA. Gently pipette up and down using a 1 mL pipette.Note: Use 4 mM EDTA-DPBS to detach cells for FACS instead of trypsin, as it preserves cell surface markers, viability and sample quality.Note: Adding BSA helps to reduce cell aggregation during FACS. This is crucial for large-scale sorting (where cells will be sorted for several hours) but may be optional for small-scale sorting.d.Transfer the cell suspension into a 1.5 mL low-protein-binding tubes.Note: Low-binding tubes are essential, as fixation and permeabilization will make cells sticky.e.Without centrifugation, fix cells by adding 37% formaldehyde to achieve a final concentration of 1% v/v.f.Incubate with gentle orbital rotation at room temperature (20°C–25°C) for 10 min.Note: This protocol is designed for subsequent gDNA extraction. For optimal genomic DNA quality, it is important not to exceed the formaldehyde concentration or incubation time.CRITICAL: Wear PPE (lab coat, safety glasses, gloves) and handle formaldehyde in a fume hood.g.Centrifuge cells at 700 ×g for 5 min at 20°C–25°C.h.Carefully aspirate supernatant. Use a 10 μL tip on a 1 mL pipette to minimize cell loss.Pause point: If needed, add DPBS with 0.2% BSA and store at 4°C with gentle rocking. The next day, centrifuge and remove the supernatant.i.Resuspend in 0.5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer and incubate with gentle orbital for 20 min at 20°C–25°C.j.Centrifuge at 700 ×g for 5 min and carefully aspirate the supernatant.k.Resuspend the cell pellet in ∼150 μL of primary antibody diluted in permeabilization/blocking buffer. Incubate for 45 min at 20°C–25°C with orbital rotation.Note: For α1AT polymer detection, use Mab2C1 at 1 μg/mL.Note: Although Mab2C1 has been well-characterized, this is the first protocol detailing its use in flow cytometry; concentration and incubation times were optimized to favor signal over background.l.Wash cells 3 times with 1 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer by centrifugation at 700 ×g for 5 min, then resuspend.m.Dilute the fluorochrome-labeled secondary antibody (Goat anti-Mouse, DyLight 633) in permeabilization/blocking buffer (1:1,000). Resuspend cells in this solution (200 μL) and incubate for 30 min at 20°C–25°C in the dark.Note: Include three controls to determinate the background fluorescence by omitting the primary, secondary, or both antibodies.n.Wash cells 4 times with 1 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer.o.Resuspend cells in ice-cold DPBS and store in the dark at 4°C. For large samples, resuspend in DPBS with 2% BSA.p.Analyze the samples by flow cytometry as soon as possible, ideally the same or following day. After 3 days, the fluorescence signal begins to decay.Note: In this study, cells were treated with a range of doxycycline concentrations, both in the presence or absence of BafilomycinA1, an inhibitor of lysosomal activity that increases α1AT polymer accumulation9 and is useful for exploring the dynamic range. The concentration of doxycycline associated with low basal levels of Mab2C1 staining, which increased conspicuously upon BafilomycinA1 treatment, was selected as the screening condition [see Supplemental Figure S1 in Ordoñez et al. (2021)1]. The doxycycline concentration chosen for screening was 10 ng/mL.Note: This example is for tetracycline-induced gene expression but can be adapted for other treatments. Consider testing fixed drug concentrations over time or a dose curve within standard working hours for high-throughput screening.CRITICAL: It is essential to gently perform multiple pipetting cycles when resuspending the cellular pellets to reduce the presence of doublets, which are excluded from flow cytometry analysis. While minor clumps may not significantly affect small-scale experiments, minimizing clumping is crucial in larger-scale screenings.

i.Resuspend in 0.5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer and incubate with gentle orbital for 20 min at 20°C–25°C.

j.Centrifuge at 700 ×g for 5 min and carefully aspirate the supernatant.

k.Resuspend the cell pellet in ∼150 μL of primary antibody diluted in permeabilization/blocking buffer. Incubate for 45 min at 20°C–25°C with orbital rotation.Note: For α1AT polymer detection, use Mab2C1 at 1 μg/mL.Note: Although Mab2C1 has been well-characterized, this is the first protocol detailing its use in flow cytometry; concentration and incubation times were optimized to favor signal over background.

p.Analyze the samples by flow cytometry as soon as possible, ideally the same or following day. After 3 days, the fluorescence signal begins to decay.Note: In this study, cells were treated with a range of doxycycline concentrations, both in the presence or absence of BafilomycinA1, an inhibitor of lysosomal activity that increases α1AT polymer accumulation9 and is useful for exploring the dynamic range. The concentration of doxycycline associated with low basal levels of Mab2C1 staining, which increased conspicuously upon BafilomycinA1 treatment, was selected as the screening condition [see Supplemental Figure S1 in Ordoñez et al. (2021)1]. The doxycycline concentration chosen for screening was 10 ng/mL.Note: This example is for tetracycline-induced gene expression but can be adapted for other treatments. Consider testing fixed drug concentrations over time or a dose curve within standard working hours for high-throughput screening.CRITICAL: It is essential to gently perform multiple pipetting cycles when resuspending the cellular pellets to reduce the presence of doublets, which are excluded from flow cytometry analysis. While minor clumps may not significantly affect small-scale experiments, minimizing clumping is crucial in larger-scale screenings.

While minor clumps may not significantly affect small-scale experiments, minimizing clumping is crucial in larger-scale screenings.

Extraction of gDNA from fixed cells at small scale

Timing: 2 weeks

Timing: 2 weeks

Formaldehyde fixes cells through a mixture of reversible (short exposure, low temperatures) and irreversible (long exposure, higher temperatures) cross-linking of amino groups in proteins and DNA. To use the gDNA for subsequent PCR amplification and cloning to create derivative sgRNA CRISPR-enriched libraries, it is essential to reverse these cross-links by treating with high salt buffer (0.5–1 M NaCl).8.Start gDNA extraction at small-scale from the suspension of fixed CHO-K1 cells collected in 1.5 low-binding tubes, with approximately 6 × 105 cells/well from 6-well plates.a.Centrifuge the cell suspension at 376 ×g at 4°C for 20 min.b.Discard the supernatant carefully.c.Resuspend the cell pellet in 0.5 mL of gDNA proteinase K (PK) lysis buffer containing 0.2 mg/mL Proteinase K.d.Incubate at 50°C 16–18 h with gently shaking to lyse the cells and digest proteins.Note: This extended incubation period allows Proteinase K to fully degrade proteins and other cellular debris, ensuring that the gDNA is free of protein contaminants.e.The following day, reverse formaldehyde cross-links by adding 35 μL of 5 M NaCl (to achieve a final concentration of 0.5 M) and incubate for another 16 h at 50°C.CRITICAL: Ensure the solution is clear; if clumps are present, pipette up and down to break them.f.Precipitate gDNA by adding an equal volume of isopropanol. Mix gently by inverting the tube several times.g.Incubate at −20°C for 30 min to 18 h to precipitate gDNA.h.Centrifuge at maximum speed (13,500-21,100 ×g) for 10 min at 4°C to pellet the DNA, then carefully remove the supernatant.i.Wash the gDNA pellet by adding 1 mL 70% ethanol, without disturbing the pellet.j.Centrifuge again at 13,500-21,100 ×g for 2 min at 4°C, then carefully remove the ethanol.k.Repeat the ethanol wash.l.Air-dry the gDNA pellet for 10–30 min until it becomes slightly translucent.m.Resuspend the gDNA pellet in 10 mM Tris pH 8 or nuclease-free water.n.Incubate at 50°C–60°C for 30 min to ensure complete dissolution, with gentle mixing.o.Measure the concentration using a nanodrop.p.Store the extracted gDNA at −20°C or −80°C until further use.

8.Start gDNA extraction at small-scale from the suspension of fixed CHO-K1 cells collected in 1.5 low-binding tubes, with approximately 6 × 105 cells/well from 6-well plates.a.Centrifuge the cell suspension at 376 ×g at 4°C for 20 min.b.Discard the supernatant carefully.c.Resuspend the cell pellet in 0.5 mL of gDNA proteinase K (PK) lysis buffer containing 0.2 mg/mL Proteinase K.d.Incubate at 50°C 16–18 h with gently shaking to lyse the cells and digest proteins.Note: This extended incubation period allows Proteinase K to fully degrade proteins and other cellular debris, ensuring that the gDNA is free of protein contaminants.e.The following day, reverse formaldehyde cross-links by adding 35 μL of 5 M NaCl (to achieve a final concentration of 0.5 M) and incubate for another 16 h at 50°C.CRITICAL: Ensure the solution is clear; if clumps are present, pipette up and down to break them.f.Precipitate gDNA by adding an equal volume of isopropanol. Mix gently by inverting the tube several times.g.Incubate at −20°C for 30 min to 18 h to precipitate gDNA.h.Centrifuge at maximum speed (13,500-21,100 ×g) for 10 min at 4°C to pellet the DNA, then carefully remove the supernatant.i.Wash the gDNA pellet by adding 1 mL 70% ethanol, without disturbing the pellet.j.Centrifuge again at 13,500-21,100 ×g for 2 min at 4°C, then carefully remove the ethanol.k.Repeat the ethanol wash.l.Air-dry the gDNA pellet for 10–30 min until it becomes slightly translucent.m.Resuspend the gDNA pellet in 10 mM Tris pH 8 or nuclease-free water.n.Incubate at 50°C–60°C for 30 min to ensure complete dissolution, with gentle mixing.o.Measure the concentration using a nanodrop.p.Store the extracted gDNA at −20°C or −80°C until further use.

Design your own screen layout

We recommend creating a detailed Gantt chart for the entire screening process, estimating the number of plates, required culture medium and timeline. This approach will enhance the overall efficiency (Figure 3).Figure 3Screen layout(A) Gantt chart illustrating the complete workflow of the CRISPR-Cas9 screen. The process starts with lentivirus production and infection using the full-size library (blue), followed by an enrichment step that generates two derivative libraries: Lib1 (green) and Lib2 (purple).(B) Table displaying estimated quantities of key tissue culture reagents used throughout the screening process, such as culture medium volume and types of flasks.

Screen layout

(A) Gantt chart illustrating the complete workflow of the CRISPR-Cas9 screen. The process starts with lentivirus production and infection using the full-size library (blue), followed by an enrichment step that generates two derivative libraries: Lib1 (green) and Lib2 (purple).

(B) Table displaying estimated quantities of key tissue culture reagents used throughout the screening process, such as culture medium volume and types of flasks.

Critical notes before starting the high-throughput screen

Before starting your screen, consider the following key parameters.9.Library Size. Determine the number of sgRNAs in your library. Whole-genome sgRNA libraries typically contain around 125,000 sgRNAs, representing about 20,000 genes, with an average of 6 sgRNAs per gene, plus controls.10.sgRNA representation (Coverage). This refers to the number of cells independently infected by a lentivirus expressing a specific sgRNA. Maintaining adequate coverage is crucial for reliable results, reducing the potential of bottlenecks and increasing confidence. A coverage of 300–500x the library size is generally recommended,10,11 with 400x being manageable for cell counts.11.Infection rate: For CRISPR screens, a low MOI of 0.3 (30% infection rate) is recommended. This ensures most cells are infected with a single viral particle carrying a sgRNA, minimizing off-target effects and toxicity.10,12,13

9.Library Size. Determine the number of sgRNAs in your library. Whole-genome sgRNA libraries typically contain around 125,000 sgRNAs, representing about 20,000 genes, with an average of 6 sgRNAs per gene, plus controls.

Library Size. Determine the number of sgRNAs in your library. Whole-genome sgRNA libraries typically contain around 125,000 sgRNAs, representing about 20,000 genes, with an average of 6 sgRNAs per gene, plus controls.

10.sgRNA representation (Coverage). This refers to the number of cells independently infected by a lentivirus expressing a specific sgRNA. A coverage of 300–500x the library size is generally recommended,10,11 with 400x being manageable for cell counts.

sgRNA representation (Coverage). This refers to the number of cells independently infected by a lentivirus expressing a specific sgRNA. Maintaining adequate coverage is crucial for reliable results, reducing the potential of bottlenecks and increasing confidence. A coverage of 300–500x the library size is generally recommended,10,11 with 400x being manageable for cell counts.

11.Infection rate: For CRISPR screens, a low MOI of 0.3 (30% infection rate) is recommended. This ensures most cells are infected with a single viral particle carrying a sgRNA, minimizing off-target effects and toxicity.10,12,13

Infection rate: For CRISPR screens, a low MOI of 0.3 (30% infection rate) is recommended. This ensures most cells are infected with a single viral particle carrying a sgRNA, minimizing off-target effects and toxicity.10,12,13

The probability of infection follows a Poisson distribution14:P(k)=[mˆ(k)∗eˆ(−m)]/k!Where P(k) is the fraction of cells infected by k virus particles, and m is the MOI.

So, with aMOI=0.2;P(1)=16.4%;P(2)=1.6%MOI=0.3;P(1)=22.2%;P(2)=3.3%MOI=0.4;P(1)=26.8%;P(2)=5.4%12.Contamination control: Preventing microbial contamination is critical. Given the numerous splitting steps and costly reagents involved, along with strict tissue culture practices (e.g., cleaning surfaces and equipment with 70% ethanol), it is advisable to supplement the medium with 50 μg/mL gentamicin and penicillin/streptomycin to further reduce bacterial contamination.

12.Contamination control: Preventing microbial contamination is critical. Given the numerous splitting steps and costly reagents involved, along with strict tissue culture practices (e.g., cleaning surfaces and equipment with 70% ethanol), it is advisable to supplement the medium with 50 μg/mL gentamicin and penicillin/streptomycin to further reduce bacterial contamination.

Contamination control: Preventing microbial contamination is critical. Given the numerous splitting steps and costly reagents involved, along with strict tissue culture practices (e.g., cleaning surfaces and equipment with 70% ethanol), it is advisable to supplement the medium with 50 μg/mL gentamicin and penicillin/streptomycin to further reduce bacterial contamination.

Reagents to prepare:

Puromycin: The stock solution is prepared by resuspending puromycin powder in ultra-pure ddH2O to a final concentration of 4 mg/mL. The solution is filtered (0.22 μm filter) and then aliquoted in 1mL aliquots in a biosafety cabinet in. Aliquots are stored at −20°C.

Polybrene: The stock solution is prepared by resuspending puromycin powder in ultra-pure ddH2O to a final concentration of 10 mg/mL in. The solution is filtered (0.22 μm filter) and then aliquoted in 1 mL aliquots in a biosafety cabinet. Aliquots are stored at −20°C.Critical equipmentAgilent high sensitivity DNA kitAgilentCat #5067-4627Gene Pulser MXcell Electroporation SystemBi-RadCat #165-2670Illumina NextSeq 4000IlluminaFigure 4Example of virus titration by BFP expression through flow cytometry(A) Two-dimensional density plots of blue fluorescent protein (BFP, infection marker) and forward scatter height (FSC-H) signals examined in recipient CHO-K1 cells, uninfected or infected with increasing amounts of lentivirus to determine the required virus amount for achieving a multiplicity of infection (MOI) of 0.3.(B) Calibration curve derived from the known percentage of BFP at known quantities of lentivirus used in “3A”, fitted to a linear function.(C) Two-dimensional density plots of BFP and FSC-H signals indicating puromycin selection efficiency after 5 days of puromycin selection.Figure 5Results of the enrichment method from fixed cells to identify modifiers of intracellular levels of α1-antitrypsin (α1AT) polymersTwo-dimensional density plots of intracellular α1AT polymers (stained with Mab2C1) and blue fluorescent protein (BFP, transduction marker) signals in α1ATH334D-expressing cells before and after transduction with Lib0 (unenriched full-size library) and successively enriched library 2 (Lib2). The boxed areas include cells sorted for genomic analysis: ‘brightest’ (B) and ‘medium-bright’ (M).Figure 6Schematic diagram of the cloning PCR amplification strategy to generate derivative enriched libraries from fixed cellsGenomic DNA (gDNA) is extracted from pools of sorted cells exhibiting the phenotype of interest (in our case brightest Mab2C1 polymer signal). This gDNA is subjected to PCR amplification targeting the integrated sgRNA region using specific primers (Pr2182 and Pr1758). The resulting 535 bp PCR product is then digested with MluI and BamHI to generate a 382 bp sgRNA insert, which is ligated into the parental lentiviral backbone (pKLV-U6gRNA(BbsI)-PGKpuro2ABFP) at a ratio 3:1, generating new lentiviral sgRNA enriched libraries denoted as Lib1 and Lib2.

Critical equipmentAgilent high sensitivity DNA kitAgilentCat #5067-4627Gene Pulser MXcell Electroporation SystemBi-RadCat #165-2670Illumina NextSeq 4000Illumina

Critical equipment

Example of virus titration by BFP expression through flow cytometry

(A) Two-dimensional density plots of blue fluorescent protein (BFP, infection marker) and forward scatter height (FSC-H) signals examined in recipient CHO-K1 cells, uninfected or infected with increasing amounts of lentivirus to determine the required virus amount for achieving a multiplicity of infection (MOI) of 0.3.

(B) Calibration curve derived from the known percentage of BFP at known quantities of lentivirus used in “3A”, fitted to a linear function.

(C) Two-dimensional density plots of BFP and FSC-H signals indicating puromycin selection efficiency after 5 days of puromycin selection.

Results of the enrichment method from fixed cells to identify modifiers of intracellular levels of α1-antitrypsin (α1AT) polymers

Two-dimensional density plots of intracellular α1AT polymers (stained with Mab2C1) and blue fluorescent protein (BFP, transduction marker) signals in α1ATH334D-expressing cells before and after transduction with Lib0 (unenriched full-size library) and successively enriched library 2 (Lib2). The boxed areas include cells sorted for genomic analysis: ‘brightest’ (B) and ‘medium-bright’ (M).

Schematic diagram of the cloning PCR amplification strategy to generate derivative enriched libraries from fixed cells

Genomic DNA (gDNA) is extracted from pools of sorted cells exhibiting the phenotype of interest (in our case brightest Mab2C1 polymer signal). This gDNA is subjected to PCR amplification targeting the integrated sgRNA region using specific primers (Pr2182 and Pr1758). The resulting 535 bp PCR product is then digested with MluI and BamHI to generate a 382 bp sgRNA insert, which is ligated into the parental lentiviral backbone (pKLV-U6gRNA(BbsI)-PGKpuro2ABFP) at a ratio 3:1, generating new lentiviral sgRNA enriched libraries denoted as Lib1 and Lib2.

Step-by-step method details

High-scale lentiviral production

Timing: 1.5–2 weeks Timing: 5 daysfor step 1 Timing: 4–6 daysfor step 2 Note: To produce sufficient lentivirus for large-scale cell transduction, aiming for at least 400-fold coverage of a library, use multiple T175 flasks. 1.Transfect HEK 293T cellsa.Plate 1.8 × 107 HEK 293T into 6 x T175 flasks, each with a total volume of 30 mL, to achieve 50% confluence by the next day.b.The following day, replace the medium with 40 mL of HEK 293T complete medium. For each T175 flask, co-transfect using: 20 μg of library lentiviral plasmid, 14.9 μg of psPAX2 and 10.1 μg of pMD2.G lentiviral packing plasmids using 3 μL TransIT-293 Transfection Reagent/μg DNA:i.Warm TransIT-293 Reagent to 20°C–25°C and vortex gently before using.ii.Dilute the DNA plasmids in 1640 μL Opti-MEM reduced-serum medium.iii.Add 135 μL TransIT-293 Reagent to the plasmid mix, then pipette gently to mix completely and incubate at 20°C–25°C for 15–30 min.iv.Add the TransIT-293 Reagent:DNA complexes drop-wise to each flask.v.Gently rock the culture to mix.c.The next day, replace the medium with 30 mL HEK 293T medium containing 1% BSA and incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C.d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect the first lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 1) into several 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tubes.i.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet debris.ii.Filter through a 0.45 μm filter.iii.Store at 4°C. Add another 30 mL complete medium with 1% BSA.iv.Incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C with 5% CO2.e.After 72 h post-transfection, collect the second lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 2) as in step “1.d” and combine with the Virus collection 1.f.Store the combined Virus collection at 4°C in a 500 mL bottle wrapped in foil while you determine the lentiviral titer.2.Determinate lentiviral titer.CRITICAL: To accurately determine the amount of virus required for a specific MOI, perform small-scale infections with the pool of lentivirus before full library infections.a.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1-Cas9 stable recipient cells per well of 6-well plates, using a total of 2.1 × 106 cells across 13 wells.b.After 24 h, replace the medium with 1.6 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium containing 2 μg/mL PB and gradually increase the virus volumes up to 600 μL. Keep one well untransduced as a control.c.After 24 h, discard the lentivirus-containing medium, wash twice with 1 mL DPBS and add 0.5 mL of trypsin to dissociate the cells.d.Incubate at 37°C for 3–5 min.e.Neutralize the trypsin with 0.5 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium and gently pipette to break up aggregates.f.Split each condition 1:2 into two 100 mm plates: one for puromycin selection and another for assessing blue fluorescent protein (BFP) expression.Note: This library plasmid (pKLV-U6gRNA(BbsI)-PGKpuro2ABFP) contains two selection markers, puromycin and BFP, allowing us to assess the transduction rate via flow cytometry. The fluorescent reporter greatly aids in determining lentiviral titer.g.After 24 h, add 8 μg/mL puromycin to one set of plates, leaving the other set untreated for BFP testing. Maintain puromycin selection for 7 days, changing the medium every 2–3 days.Note: After 4 days of puromycin treatment, you should observe an increase in dying cells.h.After 96 h post-transduction, assess BFP fluorescence by flow cytometry to determine infection rates (Figure 4A).i.Create a “titration virus standard curve” (Figure 4B) and calculate the quantity of virus required to achieve 20%–30% transduction rate (MOI: 0.3) for the large-scale screen.Note: Calculate the virus volume needed for large-scale infection based on both medium volume and cell count. Maintain the cell-to-medium ratio for accurate estimations of viral input. After storing virus at 4°C for 4–6 days, use about 1.1 times the calculated volume for the larger infection to account for potential losses.Note: If a low titer of lentivirus is obtained, refer to the Troubleshooting 1.

Timing: 1.5–2 weeks

Timing: 5 daysfor step 1

Timing: 4–6 daysfor step 2

Note: To produce sufficient lentivirus for large-scale cell transduction, aiming for at least 400-fold coverage of a library, use multiple T175 flasks.

1.Transfect HEK 293T cellsa.Plate 1.8 × 107 HEK 293T into 6 x T175 flasks, each with a total volume of 30 mL, to achieve 50% confluence by the next day.b.The following day, replace the medium with 40 mL of HEK 293T complete medium. For each T175 flask, co-transfect using: 20 μg of library lentiviral plasmid, 14.9 μg of psPAX2 and 10.1 μg of pMD2.G lentiviral packing plasmids using 3 μL TransIT-293 Transfection Reagent/μg DNA:i.Warm TransIT-293 Reagent to 20°C–25°C and vortex gently before using.ii.Dilute the DNA plasmids in 1640 μL Opti-MEM reduced-serum medium.iii.Add 135 μL TransIT-293 Reagent to the plasmid mix, then pipette gently to mix completely and incubate at 20°C–25°C for 15–30 min.iv.Add the TransIT-293 Reagent:DNA complexes drop-wise to each flask.v.Gently rock the culture to mix.c.The next day, replace the medium with 30 mL HEK 293T medium containing 1% BSA and incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C.d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect the first lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 1) into several 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tubes.i.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet debris.ii.Filter through a 0.45 μm filter.iii.Store at 4°C. Add another 30 mL complete medium with 1% BSA.iv.Incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C with 5% CO2.e.After 72 h post-transfection, collect the second lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 2) as in step “1.d” and combine with the Virus collection 1.f.Store the combined Virus collection at 4°C in a 500 mL bottle wrapped in foil while you determine the lentiviral titer.

Transfect HEK 293T cellsa.Plate 1.8 × 107 HEK 293T into 6 x T175 flasks, each with a total volume of 30 mL, to achieve 50% confluence by the next day.b.The following day, replace the medium with 40 mL of HEK 293T complete medium. For each T175 flask, co-transfect using: 20 μg of library lentiviral plasmid, 14.9 μg of psPAX2 and 10.1 μg of pMD2.G lentiviral packing plasmids using 3 μL TransIT-293 Transfection Reagent/μg DNA:i.Warm TransIT-293 Reagent to 20°C–25°C and vortex gently before using.ii.Dilute the DNA plasmids in 1640 μL Opti-MEM reduced-serum medium.iii.Add 135 μL TransIT-293 Reagent to the plasmid mix, then pipette gently to mix completely and incubate at 20°C–25°C for 15–30 min.iv.Add the TransIT-293 Reagent:DNA complexes drop-wise to each flask.v.Gently rock the culture to mix.c.The next day, replace the medium with 30 mL HEK 293T medium containing 1% BSA and incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C.d.After 48 h post-transfection, collect the first lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 1) into several 50 mL polypropylene centrifuge tubes.i.Centrifuge at 845 ×g at 4°C for 10 min to pellet debris.ii.Filter through a 0.45 μm filter.iii.Store at 4°C. Add another 30 mL complete medium with 1% BSA.iv.Incubate for an additional 24 h at 37°C with 5% CO2.e.After 72 h post-transfection, collect the second lentiviral-containing medium (Virus collection 2) as in step “1.d” and combine with the Virus collection 1.f.Store the combined Virus collection at 4°C in a 500 mL bottle wrapped in foil while you determine the lentiviral titer.

Store the combined Virus collection at 4°C in a 500 mL bottle wrapped in foil while you determine the lentiviral titer.

2.Determinate lentiviral titer.CRITICAL: To accurately determine the amount of virus required for a specific MOI, perform small-scale infections with the pool of lentivirus before full library infections.a.Plate 1.6 × 105 CHO-K1-Cas9 stable recipient cells per well of 6-well plates, using a total of 2.1 × 106 cells across 13 wells.b.After 24 h, replace the medium with 1.6 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium containing 2 μg/mL PB and gradually increase the virus volumes up to 600 μL. Keep one well untransduced as a control.c.After 24 h, discard the lentivirus-containing medium, wash twice with 1 mL DPBS and add 0.5 mL of trypsin to dissociate the cells.d.Incubate at 37°C for 3–5 min.e.Neutralize the trypsin with 0.5 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium and gently pipette to break up aggregates.f.Split each condition 1:2 into two 100 mm plates: one for puromycin selection and another for assessing blue fluorescent protein (BFP) expression.Note: This library plasmid (pKLV-U6gRNA(BbsI)-PGKpuro2ABFP) contains two selection markers, puromycin and BFP, allowing us to assess the transduction rate via flow cytometry. The fluorescent reporter greatly aids in determining lentiviral titer.g.After 24 h, add 8 μg/mL puromycin to one set of plates, leaving the other set untreated for BFP testing. Maintain puromycin selection for 7 days, changing the medium every 2–3 days.Note: After 4 days of puromycin treatment, you should observe an increase in dying cells.h.After 96 h post-transduction, assess BFP fluorescence by flow cytometry to determine infection rates (Figure 4A).i.Create a “titration virus standard curve” (Figure 4B) and calculate the quantity of virus required to achieve 20%–30% transduction rate (MOI: 0.3) for the large-scale screen.Note: Calculate the virus volume needed for large-scale infection based on both medium volume and cell count. Maintain the cell-to-medium ratio for accurate estimations of viral input. After storing virus at 4°C for 4–6 days, use about 1.1 times the calculated volume for the larger infection to account for potential losses.Note: If a low titer of lentivirus is obtained, refer to the Troubleshooting 1.

Genome-wide screen: Infection full size library and selection

Timing: Approximately 4–5 weeks CRITICAL: Ensure all reagents and media are resuspended and aliquoted before starting. CRITICAL: It is essential to daily monitor the cells for contamination and proper density. 3.Day 1: Count and seed cells.a.Plate 1.05 × 108 CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D Cas9 recipient cells into 7 × T875 5-multi-layer flasks, bringing to a total volume of 150 mL per flask.i.Add 75 mL of medium, followed by 75 mL of the total 2x cell master mix.ii.Carefully inverted the multi-layer flasks 10 times to equally distributed the cells across all layers. Refer to Falcon Muti-flask guidelines: https://www.corning.com/catalog/cls/documents/product-information-sheets/CLS-DL-CC-024.pdf. https://www.scientificlabs.co.uk/handlers/libraryFiles.ashx?filename=White_Paper_3_356275_A.pdf Note: Multi-layer flasks are recommended for simplifying the culture of large cell populations. We recommend preparing a 2x master mix of cells.CRITICAL: T875 multi-flasks are too wide for standard inverted microscopes. Consider using T175 flasks or 100 mm dishes while maintaining the same cell/cm2 ratio for proper monitoring. Alternatively, use microscope-compatible 3-layer flasks.b.Plate 3 × 106 CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells in one T175 flask for density control.c.Plate 3 × 106 CHO-K1 Tet-on_α1ATH334D cells in three 100 mm plates: one for density control, one for puromycin selection and one for BFP expression.CRITICAL: Keep untransduced cells as a control group throughout the screen.4.Day 2: Infect with CHO-sgRNA CRISPR-Cas9 KO library at full coverage.a.After 24 h, visualize control plates (T175 flasks and 100 mm dishes) under an inverted microscope to assess confluency and viability. Total cells in 7 x T875 should double to ∼2.1 × 108 (1.05 × 108 × 2) to ensure 400-fold coverage (library size: 125,030 sgRNAs) with an infection rate of ∼30%.Numberofcellsrequiredtostartthescreen=(SizeofsgRNAlibraryxCoverage)×/MOI(125,03x400)/0.3=1.7x108b.Replace the medium in T875 flasks with 150 mL medium supplemented with 2 μg/mL PB and sgRNA virus to achieve an MOI of 0.3.c.Gently invert flasks to mix.d.Incubate for 24 h at 37°C.5.Day 3: Resuspend and split cells from multi-layer flasks.CRITICAL: This day requires extensive cell culture work in a Cat II hood. Prepare all medium in advance and plan for gradual flask splitting with scheduled breaks.a.Pour lentiviral medium into a waste flask with 2% v/v Virkon or Vernagel.b.Wash each flask twice with 25 mL DPBS containing 1 mM EDTA and 0.2% v/v BSA.c.Add 20 mL of pre-warmed 20% v/v trypsin, spreading it evenly across the layers and incubate at 37°C for 5 min.d.Once the cells detach, add 30 mL of medium, mix, and pour the cell suspension into a 1 L bottle medium.e.Wash the flask with another 50 mL medium and pour into the same bottle.f.Repeat for all flasks, yielding 700 mL of cell suspension.g.Without counting cells, replate as follows:i.21 x T875 multi-layer flasks: 32.4 mL per flask; final volume 150 mL each.ii.2 x T175 flasks: 6.4 mL per flask; final volume 30 mL each.iii.3 × 100 mm dishes: 2.2 mL per dish; final volume 10 mL each. Use two dishes for BFP assessment and one as a control.Note: At this stage, cells have been infected for only 24 h. Residual lentivirus particles still present a biosafety risk, making it unsafe to handle the preparation outside the biosafety hood or dispense infected samples for cell counting. Distributing 700 mL of cell suspension across 21 × T875 flasks results in approximately 33 mL per flask. To accommodate cells for other formats, use 32.4 mL per T875 flask and reserve 12.6 mL for smaller formats: five times less for T175 flasks and fifteen times less for 100 mm dishes.Note: This is a high-throughput screen, so having at least a duplicate of each format provides a backup in case something goes wrong with one of them.CRITICAL: Once again, keep untransfected cells in T175 flasks or 150 mm dishes as controls for subsequent steps.6.Day 4: Start puromycin selection.Note: Cells should be approximately 70% confluent.a.After 48 h post infection, add 300 μL of 4 mg/mL puromycin stock to each T875 multi-layer flask to achieve a final concentration of 8 μg/mL. Also, add puromycin to 2 x T175 flasks and 1 × 100 mm dish for controls.b.Incubate at 37°C.c.Replace the medium every 3 days for puromycin selection.7.Day 6: Determine MOI by BFP expressiona.Take one 100 mm dish of untransduced cells and one of transduced but not puromycin-selected cells.b.Remove the medium, wash twice with 1 mL of DPBS and add 3 mL of 4 mM EDTA-DPBS.c.Collect cells with a 1 mL pipette, gently pipetting to break up clumps and transfer to a flow cytometry tube.d.Analyze BFP expression by flow cytometry. Note: If the percentage of BFP-positive population is between 20%–40%, continue with the screen. If it is around 50%–60%, you can still proceed, but consider that there will be a higher likelihood of cells harboring more than one sgRNA during downstream validation. Note: The safe handling of transduced cells outside of a BSL-II hood depends on the specific virus and institutional guidelines, which typically recommend waiting 72–96 h post transduction. 8.Day 7: Medium change and continue puromycin selection.a.Assess puromycin selection by examining the transduced plates under the microscope.b.Change the medium to remove dead cells and refresh puromycin. Note: After 4 days of puromycin treatment, you should observe an increase in dying cells. Note: Trypsinizing and re-plating the cells in puromycin on days 4-5 increases the selection of true puromycin-resistant cells. 9.Day 10: Splitting, expansion and first freeze of infected cells.CRITICAL: This is a long day of tissue culture (splitting 21 x T875 multi-layer flasks), so it is recommended to work with a colleague and share the workload. Aim to handle 7 flasks at a time.CRITICAL: In this step, keep the transduced cells in two or three pools (A, B and C) to reduce contamination risk. For practical reasons we chose to combine the gDNA extracted from the sorted pools to create a single gDNA sample and derivative library after each round of sorting. Alternatively, it is possible to keep the pools separate, generating separate libraries for each round of enrichment, but this significantly amplifies the workload.CRITICAL: Ensure each pool contains enough transduced cells for at least 300-400-fold library coverage. Keep at least 5 × 107 cells for freezing, gDNA extraction and cell expansion.a.Aspirate medium from the T875 flasks and wash twice with 25 mL of DPBS - 1 mM EDTA - 0.2% BSA.b.Add 20 mL of pre-warmed 20% trypsin, spreading it evenly across the layers. Incubate at 37°C for 5 min.c.Once the cells detach, add 30 mL of medium, distribute properly and pour the 50 mL of cell suspension into a 1 L bottle medium.d.Wash each flask with 50 mL medium and pour it into the same bottle.e.Repeat for all 21 flasks resulting in 2,100 mL cell suspension (100 mL/flask).f.Take a small sample to calculate the percentage of BFP-positive cells via flow cytometry (Figure 4C).CRITICAL: A BFP positivity rate above 85% indicates successful puromycin selection, justifying progression to the next step. If this percentage has not been reached, continue with puromycin selection before moving forward.g.Count cells from each pool.h.Cryopreserve live cells from each pool: Cryopreserve at least 5 × 107 transduced cells in cryovials to ensure backup in case of contamination. Use a density of 1–5 × 106 cells/mL in the freezing medium, or up to 2 × 107 cells/mL for large-scale experiments.i.Freeze cellular pellets for gDNA extraction from each pool: Freeze at least 2 × 15 mL tubes with 5 × 107 cells each per pool. Since this protocol involves extracting gDNA from fixed sorted cells, the transduced-unsorted cells must be fixed in 1% formaldehyde for 10 min before freezing to match the conditions of sorted cells.i.Transfer 5 × 107 cells into each of 2 × 15 mL conical tubes.ii.Centrifuge at 135 ×g for 5 min at 20°C–25°C.iii.Aspirate the medium, wash with 10 mL DPBS, centrifuge again at 135 ×g for 5 min and remove supernatant.iv.Resuspend cellular pellets in 10 mL DPBS.v.Add 270.2 μL of 37% formaldehyde to fix the cells.vi.Incubate for 10 min with orbital rotation at 20°C–25°C.vii.Centrifuge at 135 ×g for 5 min.viii.Wash pellets with 10 mL DPBS and carefully aspirate the supernatant.ix.Store pellets at −80°C until gDNA extraction.j.Expand cells from each pool: Maintain at least 5 × 107 cells/pool for expansion and future screening steps. Plate 3.5 × 106 cells per T175 flask across 15 flasks to ensure 400-fold library coverage.Note: Calculate the required number of cells for plating to ensure adequate representation across all treatments, considering the expected percentage of sorted cells (e.g., use an Excel spreadsheet to account for the initial number of cells plated, cells doubling time and seeding format).Note: Avoid discarding cells; freeze backups and samples for gDNA extraction after each splitting.10.Day 13: Seed cells for phenotypic selection.Once transduced cells are over 85% BFP-positive or fully selected for puromycin resistance and expanded, plate them to induce the desired phenotype: in this case, increased intracellular polymer formation after CRISPR-Cas9 mutagenesis, detected by Mab2C1 staining (Figure 5).a.Aspirate the medium and wash each T175 flask with 10 mL of DPBS containing 1 mM EDTA and 0.2% BSA.b.Add 4 mL pre-warmed 20% trypsin, distribute and incubate at 37°C.c.Add 12 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium to neutralize the trypsin and detach the cells by pipetting gently.d.Transfer the suspension to low-binding polystyrene 250 mL bottles.e.Count cells for each pool (pool A and B) and the control cells, then seed as follows:i.Samples plates: For each pool, plate approximately 15 × 150 mm plates with 4.5 × 106 cells, totaling 6.75 × 107 cells (∼540-fold coverage) per pool; 30 plates to be treated with doxycycline.ii.Untreated control plate: 1 × 150 mm plate with 4.5 × 106 transduced cells (reference plate).iii.Unstained background control plate: 1 × 150 mm plate with 4.5 × 106 transduced cells that will be induced with doxycycline but not stained.iv.Uninfected untreated control plate: 1 × 150 mm plate with 4.5 × 106 uninfected cells untreated.v.Uninfected treated control plate: 1 × 150 mm plate with 4.5 × 106 uninfected cells treated.Note: To reduce the number of plates, consider seeding more cells per 150 mm plate than indicated in the cell seeding table from the cell line characterization section. In total, seed 34 × 150 mm plates: 15 plates for each pool (pool A and B) plus 4 control plates.11.Day 14: Induce α1AT polymer expression with doxycycline.a.Prepare 1,000 mL of CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium (without puromycin) with 10 ng/mL doxycycline (add 100 μL of 100 μg/mL doxycycline stock).b.Aspirate medium from the sample plates, uninfected control plate and background control plate. Wash once with DPBS, and add 30 mL of doxycycline medium per plate.c.Change the medium for the untreated plates as well.d.Incubate at 37°C for 18 h.12.Day 15: Large-scale cellular fixation.This protocol is designed to target an intracellular protein within the lumen of the endoplasmic reticulum, requiring membrane permeabilization using non-ionic detergents such as Triton X-100. To preserve cellular integrity, fixation must be performed prior to the permeabilization step.a.Aspirate the medium and wash twice with 10 mL of pre-warmed DPBS containing 1 mM EDTA and 0.2% BSA.b.Add 6 mL of pre-warmed DPBS containing 4 mM EDTA and 0.2% BSA.c.Incubate for 5–10 min at 37°C until single cells are visible.d.Transfer to several 50 mL tubes.e.Wash dishes with another 6 mL DPBS containing 4 mM EDTA and 0.2% BSA.f.Combine the cell suspension in the same collection tubes (12 mL/dish). Repeat for 34 dishes.Note: 50 mL collection tubes are used for practical reasons, as the cell strainer used in the next step, fits perfectly in these tubes.g.Pipette up and down with 1 mL pipette to achieve a single-cell suspension, at least 20 times per tube.h.Take a 10 μL sample to determine cell number and ensure they are single cells.i.Centrifuge at 135 ×g or 10 min at 20°C–25°C.j.Carefully aspirate part of the supernatant, retaining approximately 5 mL per tube and resuspend by pipetting up and down with a 1 mL pipette.k.Combine the cells and distribute into 50 mL tubes at 2–5 × 106 cells/mL.l.Filter through a 40 μm nylon mesh cell strainer, washing the filter with 5 mL of DPBS- 4 mM EDTA-0.2% BSA.m.Take another 10 μL sample to check cell number, single cells status, and cell loss.Note: Our empirical optimization suggests that cell loss during filtration can range from 10%–30%. If needed, continue pipette until single cells are visible.n.Fix cells with 1% formaldehyde by directly adding 1/36 volume of 37% formaldehyde to the tubes and rotate for 10 min at 20°C–25°C.o.Centrifuge at 184 ×g for 10 min at 20°C–25°C.p.Discard the supernatant and resuspend in 5 mL of DPBS-4 mM EDTA-0.2% BSA.q.Transfer the cell suspension to 5 mL low-binding protein tubes.r.Incubate at 4°C without rotation for staining the next day.13.Day 16: Large-scale permeabilization and intracellular polymer staining for FACS analysis.CRITICAL: Permeabilization and fixation increase cell stickiness and the formation of cell clumps (so-called doublets), reducing sorting efficiency. To mitigate this, include EDTA and BSA in the sorting buffer, maintain cell concentrations between 1–10 × 10⁶, use a 1 mL pipette for pipetting in all steps and check cells under a microscope with a coverslip.Note: To shorten the protocol, combine permeabilization and blocking into a single step using a permeabilization/blocking buffer (DPBS containing 0.1% Triton X-100 and 10% FBS).Note: Use 5 mL low-binding protein tubes for staining, sorting and pellet visualization to reduce cell loss during washes.a.Centrifuge cells at 685 ×g, 5 min at 20°C–25°C.b.Aspirate the supernatant and resuspend the cells in 5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer. Gently pipette and incubate with orbital rotation for 30 min at 20°C–25°C.c.Centrifuge at 685 ×g for 5 min and discard the supernatant.d.Resuspend the cells in permeabilization/blocking buffer containing the primary antibody (Mab2C1; 1 μg/mL). For controls, use 350 μL of 1 μg/mL antibody solution; for sorting samples, use 1,125 μL of 1 μg/mL antibody solution. Incubate for 45 min with orbital rotation at 20°C–25°C.Note: For FACS staining, typically use 1–10 μg of antibody per million cells. For example, use 1–10 μg of primary antibody in a total volume of 100 μL to 1 mL. Mab2C1 is used at 1ug/mL; but other antibodies may require titration.e.Wash the cells 3 times with 5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer by centrifugation at 685 ×g for 5 min. Resuspend the pellet gently with a 1 mL pipette after each wash.f.Prepare secondary DyLight 633 Ab (1:1,000) in permeabilization/blocking buffer. Use 350 μL for controls and 1,125 μL for samples, both at the same concentration. Incubate for 30 min with orbital rotation at 20°C–25°C in the dark.Note: Secondary antibodies for FACS staining are generally diluted from 1:100 to 1:1,000. Optimize the dilution through titration for the best signal and minimal background.g.Wash cells 3 times with 5 mL of permeabilization/blocking buffer by centrifugation at 685 ×g for 5 min. Resuspend the pellet carefully after each wash.h.Resuspend the cells in DPBS containing 2 mM EDTA and 2% BSA. Keep them in the dark at 4°C with gentle agitation.i.For sorting purposes, concentrated samples are preferable. Reduce the cell suspension volume by half by gentle centrifugation at 685 ×g for 5 min at 20°C–25°C and resuspend. Keep in the dark at 4°C with gentle agitation overnight until the next step.14.Day 17: FACS Sorting of CRISPR-mutagenized fixed cells.This protocol describes FACS using a Beckman Coulter MoFlo cell sorter, typically operated in a flow cytometry core facility. Parameters may need to be optimized by the investigator, especially if a different flow cytometer is used.a.Keep the following numbers in mind for the sorting day:i.Sorting capacity: Typically, 3 × 107 cells/h.ii.Initial sorting amount: For the first enrichment round, collect around 2% of the total sorted cells with the highest staining signal to enrich for the strongest phenotypes.iii.Number of cells to sort for 24-fold library representation: 15 × 107 cells.Numberofcellstosort=(librarysizexcoverage)/(%sortedcells/100)15x107=(125,000x24)/(2/100)iv.Number of cells to collect: 3 × 106 cells.Numberofcellstocollect=librarysizexcoverage3,000,000=125,000x24Note: Keep the cells on ice and cover them with foil to protect from light.Note: Sorting 3 million cells takes at least 5 h. However, low efficiency due to doublets can slow the process; allocate a full day to ensure sufficient recovery.b.Prepare cells for sorting:i.From step “13.i”, gently pipette each sample with a 1 mL pipette after keeping it overnight at 4°C to ensure single cells.ii.Take a 10 μL sample to verify the presence of single cells.iii.Filter the cell suspension from both pools (A and B) through a 40 μm cell strainer, collecting the filtered suspension in 50 mL tubes.iv.Transfer the filtered cells to 5 mL polypropylene round-bottom tubes, commonly used for cell sorting, and keep on ice. The cells are ready for sorting.v.Prepare 5 mL low-binding tubes with 500 μL CHO Ham’s F12 complete medium to collect 3–5 million sorted cells per pool.Note: Before sorting samples en masse, we recommend analyzing controls and a few samples by flow cytometry to determine the percentage of cells with the phenotype of interest and to design the gating strategy.Note: If low sorting efficiency is observed, refer to Troubleshooting 2.c.Set gates for sorting:i.FSC-A vs. SSC-A for live cells.ii.FSC-W vs. SSC to identify single cells and exclude doublets.iii.The fluorescent channels will depend on your strategy (e.g., Mab2C1 stained cells were detected with an excitation laser at 640 nm and a 670/14 nm emission filter).Note: Begin by running unstained samples and controls to establish appropriate gates. Sort pools A and B. The fixation process outlined in this protocol introduces convenient stopping points; if sorting both pools in one day is not possible, store the second pool in the dark at 4°C and sort it the following day.Note: Refer to Troubleshooting 3 if the BFP signal is faint during sorting.d.Keep sorted cells tubes on ice for immediate gDNA extraction.

We recommend preparing a 2x master mix of cells.CRITICAL: T875 multi-flasks are too wide for standard inverted microscopes. Consider using T175 flasks or 100 mm dishes while maintaining the same cell/cm2 ratio for proper monitoring. Alternatively, use microscope-compatible 3-layer flasks.

4.Day 2: Infect with CHO-sgRNA CRISPR-Cas9 KO library at full coverage.a.After 24 h, visualize control plates (T175 flasks and 100 mm dishes) under an inverted microscope to assess confluency and viability. Total cells in 7 x T875 should double to ∼2.1 × 108 (1.05 × 108 × 2) to ensure 400-fold coverage (library size: 125,030 sgRNAs) with an infection rate of ∼30%.Numberofcellsrequiredtostartthescreen=(SizeofsgRNAlibraryxCoverage)×/MOI(125,03x400)/0.3=1.7x108b.Replace the medium in T875 flasks with 150 mL medium supplemented with 2 μg/mL PB and sgRNA virus to achieve an MOI of 0.3.c.Gently invert flasks to mix.d.Incubate for 24 h at 37°C.
